Configuration under `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>` for Canton 3.7.0-SNAPSHOT: 965 keys. Part of the [Canton configuration reference](/global-synchronizer/reference/canton-config/overview), which explains how to read these pages.

## All options

### `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>`

<Tabs>
<Tab title="HOCON">

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer> {
  acknowledgements-conflate-window = "45 seconds"
}
```

`sequencer` supports these types: `database`, `external`, `bft-sequencer`. Pick one; each block is a complete example of that choice.

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.sequencer {
  type = database
  # testing-interceptor = <string>
  high-availability {
    # enabled = <boolean>
    keep-alive-interval = "50 milliseconds"
    offline-duration = "8 seconds"
    online-check-interval = "5 seconds"
    total-node-count = 10
    connection-pool {
      active-timeout = "15 seconds"
      health-check-period = "5 seconds"
      initial-locked-connection-timeout = "15 seconds"
      initial-must-remain-active-connection-timeout = "1 minute"
      connection {
        client-connection-check-interval = "5 seconds"
        connection-timeout = "10 seconds"
        health-check-period = "5 seconds"
        health-check-timeout = "15 seconds"
        initial-acquisition-interval = "200 milliseconds"
        initial-acquisition-max-retries = 5
        keep-alive-count = 5
        keep-alive-idle = "10 seconds"
        keep-alive-interval = "1 second"
        max-inconclusive-read-only-checks = 3
        passive-check-period = "15 seconds"
        lock {
          health-check-period = "5 seconds"
          health-check-timeout = "15 seconds"
        }
      }
    }
    exclusive-storage {
      max-connections = 2
      connection-pool {
        active-timeout = "15 seconds"
        health-check-period = "5 seconds"
        initial-locked-connection-timeout = "15 seconds"
        initial-must-remain-active-connection-timeout = "1 minute"
        connection {
          client-connection-check-interval = "5 seconds"
          connection-timeout = "10 seconds"
          health-check-period = "5 seconds"
          health-check-timeout = "15 seconds"
          initial-acquisition-interval = "200 milliseconds"
          initial-acquisition-max-retries = 5
          keep-alive-count = 5
          keep-alive-idle = "10 seconds"
          keep-alive-interval = "1 second"
          max-inconclusive-read-only-checks = 3
          passive-check-period = "15 seconds"
          lock {
            health-check-period = "5 seconds"
            health-check-timeout = "15 seconds"
          }
        }
      }
    }
  }
  pruning {
    max-pruning-batch-size = 50000
    # pruning-metric-update-interval = <duration>
    traffic-purchased-retention = "1 hour"
  }
  reader {
    checkpoint-interval = "5 seconds"
    event-generation-parallelism = 4
    payload-batch-size = 10
    payload-batch-window = "5 milliseconds"
    payload-fetch-parallelism = 2
    # polling-interval = <duration>
    read-batch-size = 100
  }
  writer {
    type = low-latency|high-throughput   # required
  }
}
```

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.sequencer {
  type = external
  config = "..."   # required
  sequencer-type = "..."   # required
  block {
    # testing-interceptor = <string>
    circuit-breaker {
      enabled = true
      messages {
        acknowledgement {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
        commitment {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
        confirmation-request {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"

          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
        lsu-sequencing-test {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
        time-proof {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
        top-up {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
        topology {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
        unexpected {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
        verdict {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
      }
    }
    reader {
      checkpoint-interval = "5 seconds"
      event-generation-parallelism = 4
      payload-batch-size = 10
      payload-batch-window = "5 milliseconds"
      payload-fetch-parallelism = 2
      # polling-interval = <duration>
      read-batch-size = 100
    }
    stream-instrumentation {
      buffer-size = 256
      is-enabled = false
    }
    throughput-cap {
      clock-tick-interval = "100 milliseconds"
      delayed-activation = true
      enabled = false
      observation-period-seconds = 60
      strict = true
      thresholds = [ ]
      update-every-ms = 100
      messages {
        confirmation-request {
          global-kbps-cap = 2000
          global-tps-cap = 10
          per-client-kbps-cap = 1000
          per-client-tps-cap = 4
        }
        confirmation-response {
          global-kbps-cap = 2000
          global-tps-cap = 10
          per-client-kbps-cap = 1000
          per-client-tps-cap = 4
        }
        topology {
          global-kbps-cap = 2000
          global-tps-cap = 10
          per-client-kbps-cap = 1000
          per-client-tps-cap = 4
        }
      }
    }
    writer {
      type = low-latency|high-throughput   # required
    }
  }
}
```

```hocon
      }
    }
    reader {
      checkpoint-interval = "5 seconds"
      event-generation-parallelism = 4
      payload-batch-size = 10
      payload-batch-window = "5 milliseconds"

      # polling-interval = <duration>

    }
    stream-instrumentation {
      buffer-size = 256
      is-enabled = false
    }
    throughput-cap {
      clock-tick-interval = "100 milliseconds"
      delayed-activation = true
      enabled = false
      observation-period-seconds = 60
      strict = true
      thresholds = [ ]

      messages {
        confirmation-request {
          global-kbps-cap = 2000
          global-tps-cap = 10
          per-client-kbps-cap = 1000

    }

      type = low-latency|high-throughput   # required
    }
  }
  config {
    availability-dissemination-patience = "5 seconds"
    availability-max-non-ordered-batches-per-node = 1000
    availability-min-proposal-creation-delay = "250 milliseconds"
    availability-number-of-attempts-of-downloading-output-fetch-before-warning = 5
    blocking-db-read-timeout = "1 minute"
    consensus-block-completion-timeout = "10 seconds"
    consensus-empty-block-creation-timeout = "500 milliseconds"
    consensus-enable-flushing-segment = true
    consensus-enable-log-end-of-epoch-progress = false
    consensus-flushing-min-blocks = 2
    consensus-new-epoch-topology-warn-timeout = "2 seconds"
    # consensus-queue-max-size = <int>
    consensus-queue-per-node-quota = 1024
    # dedicated-execution-context-divisor = <int>
    delayed-init-queue-max-size = 1024
    enable-performance-metrics = true
    # epoch-state-transfer-future-epoch-queue-max-size = <int>
    epoch-state-transfer-future-epoch-queue-per-node-quota = 1024
    epoch-state-transfer-how-many-future-epochs-to-download-in-parallel = 5
    epoch-state-transfer-retry-timeout = "4 seconds"
    epoch-state-transfer-timeout-for-future-epoch = "500 milliseconds"
    # init-query-timeout = <duration>
    # init-timeout = <duration>
    manual-vacuum-enabled = false
    max-batch-creation-interval = "100 milliseconds"
    # max-mempool-queue-size = <int>
    min-requests-in-batch = 3
    network-send-attempts = 5
    # network-send-retry-jitter-cap = <duration>
    # network-send-retry-minimum-delay = <duration>
    # output-enqueue-max-retries = <int>
    output-enqueue-max-retry-delay = "5 seconds"
    output-fetch-how-many-recipients = 2
    output-fetch-minimum-delay = "200 milliseconds"
    output-fetch-timeout = "200 milliseconds"
    output-fetch-timeout-cap = "200 milliseconds"
    output-size-of-chunk-of-epochs-to-load-at-start = 10
    # segment-length-for-pv-34 = <int>
    send-blacklist-ttl = "3 minutes"
    # view-change-timeout-override = <duration>
    batch-aggregator {
      type = batching|no-batching   # required
    }
    initial-network {
      overwrite-stored-endpoints = false
      connection-management-config {
        # connection-retry-delay-multiplier = <int>
        flow-control-buffer = 128
        flow-control-buffer-drop-newest = false
        # flow-control-ready-allowance = <int>
        initial-connection-max-delay = "500 milliseconds"
        initial-connection-retry-delay = "500 milliseconds"
        max-connection-attempts-before-warning = 30
        max-connection-retry-delay = "2 minutes"
      }
      endpoint-authentication {
        enabled = true
        auth-token {
          min-retry-interval = "500 milliseconds"
          refresh-auth-token-before-expiry = "20 seconds"
          retries = 20
          backoff {
            base = 2
            max-retry-interval = "2 minutes"
            jitter {
              type = equal|full   # required
            }
          }
        }
      }
      peer-endpoints = [
        {
          address = "..."   # required
          port = 0   # required
          channel {
            # flow-control-window = <int>
            # initial-flow-control-window = <int>
            max-inbound-message-size = 0   # required
            trace-context-propagation = enabled|disabled   # required
            keep-alive-client {
              idle-timeout = "30 minutes"
              keep-alive-without-calls = false
              time = "40 seconds"
              timeout = "15 seconds"
            }
          }
          tls-config {
            enabled = true
            client-cert {
              private-key-file = "..."   # required
              cert-chain-file {
                type = pem-file|pem-string   # required
              }
            }
            trust-collection-file {
              type = pem-file|pem-string   # required
            }
          }
        }
      ]
      server-endpoint {
        address = "..."   # required
        external-address = "..."   # required
        external-port = 0   # required
        # flow-control-window = <int>
        # initial-flow-control-window = <int>
        max-concurrent-calls-per-connection = 100000
        # max-inbound-message-size = <int>
        # port = <int>
        external-tls-config {
          enabled = true
          client-cert {
            private-key-file = "..."   # required
            cert-chain-file {
              type = pem-file|pem-string   # required
            }
          }
          trust-collection-file {
            type = pem-file|pem-string   # required
          }
        }
        keep-alive-server {
          permit-keep-alive-time = "20 seconds"
          permit-keep-alive-without-calls = false
          time = "40 seconds"
          timeout = "20 seconds"
        }
        limits {
          throttle-logging-rate-per-second = 10
          warn-on-undefined-limits = false
          active {
            <key> = [ ]
          }
        }
        tls {
          # ciphers = [ ... ]
          enable-cert-revocation-checking = false
          minimum-server-protocol-version = "TLSv1.2"
          private-key-file = "..."   # required
          cert-chain-file {
            type = pem-file|pem-string   # required
          }
          client-auth {
            type = require|optional|none   # required
          }
          trust-collection-file {
            type = pem-file|pem-string   # required
          }
        }
      }
    }
    leader-selection-policy-config-for-pv-34 {
      how-many-can-we-blacklist = num-faults-tolerated|no-blacklisting   # required
      how-long-to-blacklist {
        type = linear|linear-with-parameters|exponential|no-blacklisting   # required
      }
    }
    sequencer-core-subscription-config {
      pause-orderer-threshold-buffer-size = 5000
      pekko-queue-source-buffer-size = 5000
      resume-orderer-threshold-buffer-size = 1000
    }
    standalone {
      max-batches-per-block-proposal = 0   # required
      max-requests-in-batch = 0   # required
      pbft-view-change-timeout = "30 seconds"   # required
      segment-length = 0   # required
      signing-private-key-proto-file = "/path/to/file"   # required
      signing-public-key-proto-file = "/path/to/file"   # required
      this-sequencer-id = "..."   # required
      blacklist-leader-selection-policy-config {
        how-many-can-we-blacklist = num-faults-tolerated|no-blacklisting   # required
        how-long-to-blacklist {
          type = linear|linear-with-parameters|exponential|no-blacklisting   # required
        }
      }
      peers = [
        {
          sequencer-id = "..."   # required
          signing-public-key-proto-file = "/path/to/file"   # required
        }
      ]
      test-slowdown {
        post-ordering-delay {
          type = constant-distribution|weighted-distribution|linear-distribution|power-distribution   # required
        }
        send-delay {
          default-delay-distribution {
            type = constant-distribution|weighted-distribution|linear-distribution|power-distribution   # required
          }
          delays-by-recipients = [
            {
              instance-names = [ ]   # required
              # probability-of-grpc-send-accepted-by-flow-control = <number>
              # probability-of-grpc-send-success = <number>
              delay-distribution {
                type = constant-distribution|weighted-distribution|linear-distribution|power-distribution   # required
              }
            }
          ]
        }
        topology-delay {
          # broadcast-request-probability = <number>
          # pending-topology-changes-probability = <number>
          # possible-ordering-topology-change-in-request-probability = <number>
          get-ordering-topology-delay {
            type = constant-distribution|weighted-distribution|linear-distribution|power-distribution   # required
          }
          request-inspection-delay {
            type = constant-distribution|weighted-distribution|linear-distribution|power-distribution   # required
          }
        }
      }
    }
    storage {
      type = memory|h2|postgres   # required
    }
  }
  # config.initial-network.server-endpoint.tls.client-auth.admin-client.cert-chain-file.type takes a type of its own, enumerated below
}
```

`storage` supports these types: `memory`, `h2`, `postgres`. Pick one; each block is a complete example of that choice.

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.storage {
  type = memory
  parameters {
    connection-timeout = "5 seconds"
    fail-fast-on-startup = true
    failed-to-fatal-delay = "5 minutes"
    # max-connections = <int>
    migrate-and-start = false
    migrations-paths = [ ]
    repeatable-migrations-paths = [ ]
    unsafe-baseline-on-migrate = false
    unsafe-clean-on-validation-error = false
    # warn-on-slow-query = <duration>
    warn-on-slow-query-interval = "5 seconds"
    connection-allocation {
      # num-ledger-api = <int>
      # num-reads = <int>
      # num-writes = <int>
    }
    partitions {
      initial-bft-orderer-tables-partition-size = 1500
    }
  }
}
```

`admin-api.auth-services[]` supports these types: `wildcard`, `unsafe-jwt-hmac-256`, `jwt-rs-256-crt`, `jwt-es-256-crt`, `jwt-es-512-crt`, `jwt-jwks`, `party-jwt`. Each block shows one entry; a list may hold entries of different types.

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.admin-api.auth-services = [
  {
    type = wildcard
    # takes no further keys
  }
]
```

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.admin-api.auth-services = [
  {
    type = unsafe-jwt-hmac-256
    access-level = wildcard
    # max-token-life = <duration>
    privileged = false
    secret = "..."   # required
    # target-audience = <string>
    # target-scope = <string>
    users = [
      {
        allowed-services = [ ]   # required
        user-id = "..."   # required
      }
    ]
  }
]
```

`crypto.kms` supports these types: `driver`, `aws`, `gcp`. Pick one; each block is a complete example of that choice.

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.crypto.kms {
  type = driver
  config = { }   # required
  health-check-period = "10 seconds"
  name = "..."   # required
  retries {
    create-key-check {
      initial-delay = "30 seconds"   # required
      max-delay = "30 seconds"   # required
      max-retries = 0   # required
    }
    failures {
      initial-delay = "30 seconds"   # required
      max-delay = "30 seconds"   # required
      max-retries = 0   # required
    }
  }
}
```

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.crypto.kms {
  type = aws
  audit-logging = false

    }
  }
}

    }
  }
```
```hocon
]
```

`sequencer.config.standalone.test-slowdown.send-delay.delays-by-recipients[].delay-distribution` supports these types: `constant-distribution`, `weighted-distribution`, `linear-distribution`, `power-distribution`. Pick one; each block is a complete example of that choice.

```hocon
]
```

```hocon
    }
  }
]
```

### `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.admin-api`

<Tabs>
<Tab title="HOCON">

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.admin-api {
  address = "127.0.0.1"
  # flow-control-window = <int>
  initial-flow-control-window = 1048576
  max-concurrent-calls-per-connection = 100000
  max-inbound-message-size = 10485760
  max-token-lifetime = "5 minutes"
  port = 0   # required
  admin-token-config {
    act-as-any-party-claim = false
    admin-claim = false
    admin-token-duration = "5 minutes"
    # fixed-admin-token = <string>
  }
  auth-services = [
    {
      type = wildcard|unsafe-jwt-hmac-256|jwt-rs-256-crt|jwt-es-256-crt|jwt-es-512-crt|jwt-jwks|party-jwt   # required
    }
  ]
  jwks-cache-config {
    auto-refresh-after = "0 seconds"
    cache-expiration = "5 minutes"
    cache-max-size = 1000
    connection-timeout = "10 seconds"
    read-timeout = "10 seconds"
  }
  jwt-timestamp-leeway {
    # default = <int>
    # expires-at = <int>
    # issued-at = <int>
    # not-before = <int>
  }
  keep-alive-server {
    permit-keep-alive-time = "20 seconds"
    permit-keep-alive-without-calls = false
    time = "40 seconds"
    timeout = "20 seconds"
  }
  limits {
    throttle-logging-rate-per-second = 10
    warn-on-undefined-limits = false
    active {
      <key> = [ ]
    }
  }
  tls {
    # ciphers = [ ... ]
    enable-cert-revocation-checking = false
    minimum-server-protocol-version = "TLSv1.2"
    private-key-file = "..."   # required
    cert-chain-file {
      type = pem-file|pem-string   # required
    }
    client-auth {
      type = require|optional|none   # required
    }
    trust-collection-file {
      type = pem-file|pem-string   # required
    }
  }
}
```

`auth-services[]` supports these types: `wildcard`, `unsafe-jwt-hmac-256`, `jwt-rs-256-crt`, `jwt-es-256-crt`, `jwt-es-512-crt`, `jwt-jwks`, `party-jwt`. Each block shows one entry; a list may hold entries of different types.

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.admin-api.auth-services = [
  {
    type = wildcard
    # takes no further keys
  }
]
```

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.admin-api.auth-services = [
  {
    type = unsafe-jwt-hmac-256
    access-level = wildcard
    # max-token-life = <duration>
    privileged = false
    secret = "..."   # required
    # target-audience = <string>
    # target-scope = <string>
    users = [
      {
        allowed-services = [ ]   # required
        user-id = "..."   # required
      }
    ]
  }
]
```

```hocon
    ]
  }
]
```

### `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.crypto`

<Tabs>
<Tab title="HOCON">

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.crypto {
  enable-experimental = false
  provider = jce
  encryption {
    algorithms {
      # allowed = [ ... ]
      # default = <generic>
    }
    keys {
      # allowed = [ ... ]
      # default = <generic>
    }
  }
  hash {
    # allowed = [ ... ]
    # default = <generic>
  }
  kms {
    type = driver|aws|gcp   # required
    retries {
      create-key-check {
        initial-delay = "30 seconds"   # required
        max-delay = "30 seconds"   # required
        max-retries = 0   # required
      }
      failures {
        initial-delay = "30 seconds"   # required
        max-delay = "30 seconds"   # required
        max-retries = 0   # required
      }
    }
  }
  parallelism {
    encryption-parallelism = 10
    signature-verification-parallelism = 10
  }
  pbkdf {
    # allowed = [ ... ]
    # default = <generic>
  }
  private-key-store {
    encryption {
      type = kms   # required
      reverted = false
      # wrapper-key-id = <string>
    }
  }
  session-signing-keys {
    cut-off-duration = "1 minute"
    disable-bound-checks = false
    enabled = false   # required
    key-eviction-period = "10 minutes"
    key-validity-duration = "5 minutes"
    signing-algorithm-spec = ed-25519
    signing-key-spec = ec-curve-25519
    tolerance-shift-duration = "2 minutes"
  }
  signing {
    algorithms {
      # allowed = [ ... ]
      # default = <generic>
    }
    keys {
      # allowed = [ ... ]
      # default = <generic>
    }
  }
  symmetric {
    # allowed = [ ... ]
    # default = <generic>
  }
}
```

`kms` supports these types: `driver`, `aws`, `gcp`. Pick one; each block is a complete example of that choice.

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.crypto.kms {
  type = driver
  config = { }   # required
  health-check-period = "10 seconds"
  name = "..."   # required
  retries {
    create-key-check {
      initial-delay = "30 seconds"   # required
      max-delay = "30 seconds"   # required
      max-retries = 0   # required
    }
    failures {
      initial-delay = "30 seconds"   # required
      max-delay = "30 seconds"   # required
      max-retries = 0   # required
    }
  }
}
```

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.crypto.kms {
  type = aws
  audit-logging = false
  disable-ssl-verification = false
  # endpoint-override = <string>
  multi-region-key = false
  region = "..."   # required
  custom-tags {
    <custom-tag> = [ ]
  }
  retries {
    create-key-check {
      initial-delay = "30 seconds"   # required
      max-delay = "30 seconds"   # required
      max-retries = 0   # required
    }
    failures {
      initial-delay = "30 seconds"   # required
      max-delay = "30 seconds"   # required
      max-retries = 0   # required
    }
  }
}
```

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.crypto.kms {
  type = gcp
  audit-logging = false
  # endpoint-override = <string>
  key-ring-id = "..."   # required
  location-id = "..."   # required
  project-id = "..."   # required
  retries {
    create-key-check {
      initial-delay = "30 seconds"   # required
      max-delay = "30 seconds"   # required
      max-retries = 0   # required
    }
    failures {
      initial-delay = "30 seconds"   # required
      max-delay = "30 seconds"   # required
      max-retries = 0   # required
    }
  }
}
```

`private-key-store.encryption` supports these types: `kms`. Pick one; each block is a complete example of that choice.

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.crypto.private-key-store.encryption {
  type = kms
  reverted = false
  # wrapper-key-id = <string>
}
```

|---|---|---|---|---|

| **encryption** |  |  |  | Stores the configuration of the encryption scheme. |
|     **algorithms** |  |  |  | Configures the optional default and allowed schemes of kind S. |
|         `allowed` | array of S |  | _unset_ | The optional allowed schemes to use. If none is specified, all the provider's supported schemes of kind S are allowed. |
|         `default` | depends on use (S) |  | _unset_ | The optional scheme to use. If none is specified, use the provider's default scheme of kind S. |
|     **keys** |  |  |  | Configures the optional default and allowed schemes of kind S. |
|         `allowed` | array of S |  | _unset_ | The optional allowed schemes to use. If none is specified, all the provider's supported schemes of kind S are allowed. |
|         `default` | depends on use (S) |  | _unset_ | The optional scheme to use. If none is specified, use the provider's default scheme of kind S. |
| **hash** |  |  |  | Configures the optional default and allowed schemes of kind S. |
|     `allowed` | array of S |  | _unset_ | The optional allowed schemes to use. If none is specified, all the provider's supported schemes of kind S are allowed. |
|     `default` | depends on use (S) |  | _unset_ | The optional scheme to use. If none is specified, use the provider's default scheme of kind S. |
| **kms** |  |  |  |  |
|     `type` | one of `driver`, `aws`, `gcp` | yes |  | Choose one type — see [KmsConfig](#kmsconfig). |
|     **retries** |  |  |  | Retry configuration for KMS operations |
|         **create-key-check** |  |  |  | Exponential backoff configuration for retries of operations |
|             `initial-delay` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | yes |  | initial delay before the first retry |
|             `max-delay` | duration (NonNegativeDuration) | yes |  | max delay between retries |
|             `max-retries` | int | yes |  | max number of retries |
|         **failures** |  |  |  | Exponential backoff configuration for retries of operations |
|             `initial-delay` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) | yes |  | initial delay before the first retry |
|             `max-delay` | duration (NonNegativeDuration) | yes |  | max delay between retries |
|             `max-retries` | int | yes |  | max number of retries |
| **kms** `type = driver` |  |  |  | A KMS configuration for an external KMS driver. |
|     `config` | config-value | yes |  | The driver specific raw config section |
|     `health-check-period` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) |  | `10 seconds` | How long to wait between health checks of the KMS driver |
|     `name` | string | yes |  | The name of the external KMS driver |
| **kms** `type = aws` |  |  |  | Stores the configuration for AWS KMS. |
|     `audit-logging` | boolean |  | `false` | when enabled, all calls to KMS will be logged. Defaults to false. |
|     `disable-ssl-verification` | boolean |  | `false` | When set to true, SSL verification is disabled. Mostly for testing purposes. Can only be used if non-standard-config is enabled. |
|     `endpoint-override` | string |  | _unset_ | the [optional] endpoint for a proxy to be used by the KMS client. |
|     `multi-region-key` | boolean |  | `false` | flag to enable multiRegion keys (Canton will generate single region keys by default) |
|     `region` | string | yes |  | defines the AWS region to be use (e.g. us-east-1) |
|     **custom-tags** |  |  |  |  |
|         `<custom-tag>` | string |  | `[]` | custom tags to attach to AWS KMS keys created by Canton. |
| **kms** `type = gcp` |  |  |  | Stores the configuration for GCP KMS. |
|     `audit-logging` | boolean |  | `false` | when enabled, all calls to KMS will be logged. Defaults to false. |
|     `endpoint-override` | string |  | _unset_ | the [optional] endpoint for a proxy to be used by the KMS client. |
|     `key-ring-id` | string | yes |  | defines a key-ring to where keys will be added. This can be created as a multi-region key-ring, which enables multi-region keys |
|     `location-id` | string | yes |  | defines the GCP region to use (e.g. us-east1) |
|     `project-id` | string | yes |  | defines a GCP project to use (e.g. gcp-kms-testing) |
| **parallelism** |  |  |  | Configuration for crypto-related parallelism settings. |
|     `encryption-parallelism` | int (PositiveInt) |  | `10` |  |
|     `signature-verification-parallelism` | int (PositiveInt) |  | `10` | Maximum number of signature verification tasks to run in parallel per signed payload (when multiple signatures are provided). |
| **pbkdf** |  |  |  | Configures the optional default and allowed schemes of kind S. |
|     `allowed` | array of S |  | _unset_ | The optional allowed schemes to use. If none is specified, all the provider's supported schemes of kind S are allowed. |
|     `default` | depends on use (S) |  | _unset_ | The optional scheme to use. If none is specified, use the provider's default scheme of kind S. |
| **private-key-store** |  |  |  | Stores the configuration for a private store |
|     **encryption** |  |  |  | Encapsulates possible configurations for different encrypted private stores |
|         `reverted` | boolean |  | `false` | when true decrypts the stored private keys and stores them in clear, disabling the encrypted crypto private key store in the process |
|         `type` | one of `kms` | yes |  | Choose one type — see [EncryptedPrivateStoreConfig](#encryptedprivatestoreconfig). Encapsulates possible configurations for different encrypted private stores |
|         `wrapper-key-id` | string (KmsKeyId) |  | _unset_ | defines key identifier for the wrapper key (e.g. ARN for AWS SDK). When it's None Canton will either create a new key or use a previous existent key |
| **session-signing-keys** |  |  |  | Configuration for enabling session signing keys with a specified validity period. |
|     `cut-off-duration` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) |  | `1 minute` | A cut-off duration is applied only when we need to sign something without knowing the exact topology timestamp that will later be used for verification (e.g., when using a currentSnapshotApproximation). The cutoff measures the amount of clock skew that can be tolerated and therefore should be longer than our `ledgerRecordTimeTolerance`. The participant makes a guess t0 at the topology timestamp for verification and will use an existing session signing key k only if the interval [t0 - cutoff, t0 + cutoff] lies fully within k’s validity period. If the full validity interval is known a priori (e.g., we know the maximum sequencing time for the message we want to sign), we need to ensure that we use an existing session signing key k only if the interval [t0 - cutoff, endValidityPeriod] is fully covered. As a result, newly created session signing keys must have a validity period that starts at least cutoffDuration before ts. A typical example of this uncertainty is signing submission requests, where the sequencer-assigned timestamp is unknown in advance. If a new session key is created only after the previous key’s validity interval ends, multiple submissions may fail verification if any clock skew exists between the nodes. |
|     `disable-bound-checks` | boolean |  | `false` | Flag to disable parameter bounds, allowing any values. Should only be used for testing and requires `nonStandardConfig` to be enabled. |
|     `enabled` | boolean | yes | `false` † | Enables the usage of session signing keys in the protocol. |
|     `key-eviction-period` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) |  | `10 minutes` | This defines how long the private session signing key remains in memory. This is distinct from the validity period in the sense that we can be asked to sign arbitrarily old timestamps, and so we want to persist the key for longer times so we can re-use it. The eviction period should be longer than keyValidityDuration and at least as long as the majority of confirmation request decision latencies (for the mediator) or confirmation request response latencies (for participants). It should also be longer than a participant's response timeout. For example, a participant may create a submission at time ts0, which is sequenced at ts1. Later, it may need to send a response at ts2 using the original timestamp ts1. Under normal operation, ts2 − ts0 should not exceed the participant response timeout. Therefore, if the eviction period is longer than this timeout, the participant won’t need to create a new session signing key just to send the confirmation response. |
|     `key-validity-duration` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) |  | `5 minutes` | Specifies the validity duration for each session signing key. Its lifespan MUST be configured to be: keyValidityDuration &gt; 2 * cutoffDuration. The validity duration should also not be too short, so that a session signing key can be reused across, for example, multiple submission requests. |
|     `signing-algorithm-spec` | one of `ed-25519`, `ec-dsa-sha-256`, `ec-dsa-sha-384`, `ml-dsa-65` |  | `ed-25519` | Defines the signing algorithm when using session signing keys. It defaults to Ed25519. |
|     `signing-key-spec` | one of `ec-curve-25519`, `ec-p-256`, `ec-p-384`, `ec-secp-256k-1`, `ml-dsa-65` |  | `ec-curve-25519` | Defines the key scheme to use for the session signing keys. It defaults to EcCurve25519. Both algorithm and key scheme must be supported and allowed by the node. |
|     `tolerance-shift-duration` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) |  | `2 minutes` | This defines the maximum value we will attempt to use when shifting the validity interval from '[ts, ts+keyValidityDuration]' to '[ts-tolerance, ts+keyValidityDuration-tolerance]'. It MUST respect the following constraints: (1) keyValidityDuration - toleranceShiftDuration &gt;= cutoffDuration; (2) toleranceShiftDuration &gt;= cutoffDuration. What we are trying to solve with this shift is the following: Given a sequence of timestamps `tss`, find a set of intervals of fixed length `keyValidityDuration` such that each timestamp in `tss` falls within at least one interval. The session signing key selection algorithm picks intervals as it goes through the list of timestamps from left to right, without any lookahead. As such, the goal is to minimize the number of intervals needed. For example, consider the sequence of timestamps: `ts0`, `ts0-1us`, `ts0-2us`, `ts0-3us`. Without applying this shift, a new interval (i.e., a new session signing key) would be created for each of these slightly different timestamps. Shifting the interval allows us to cover multiple timestamps with a single interval, reducing the number of session keys created. This value can be adjusted: reduced if timestamps are mostly increasing, or increased if timestamps are mostly decreasing. A small note: if we know exactly until when our key must be valid (e.g., the maximum sequencing time), then we do not shift the validity interval centered on ts. Instead, we select a key validity period that takes this end validity time into account. |
| **signing** |  |  |  | Stores the configuration of the signing scheme. |
|     **algorithms** |  |  |  | Configures the optional default and allowed schemes of kind S. |
|         `allowed` | array of S |  | _unset_ | The optional allowed schemes to use. If none is specified, all the provider's supported schemes of kind S are allowed. |
|         `default` | depends on use (S) |  | _unset_ | The optional scheme to use. If none is specified, use the provider's default scheme of kind S. |
|     **keys** |  |  |  | Configures the optional default and allowed schemes of kind S. |
|         `allowed` | array of S |  | _unset_ | The optional allowed schemes to use. If none is specified, all the provider's supported schemes of kind S are allowed. |
|         `default` | depends on use (S) |  | _unset_ | The optional scheme to use. If none is specified, use the provider's default scheme of kind S. |
| **symmetric** |  |  |  | Configures the optional default and allowed schemes of kind S. |
|     `allowed` | array of S |  | _unset_ | The optional allowed schemes to use. If none is specified, all the provider's supported schemes of kind S are allowed. |
|     `default` | depends on use (S) |  | _unset_ | The optional scheme to use. If none is specified, use the provider's default scheme of kind S. |

### `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.init`

<Tabs>
<Tab title="HOCON">

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.init {
  generate-intermediate-key = false
  generate-topology-transactions-and-keys = true
  identity {
    type = external|auto|manual   # required
  }
}
```

`identity` supports these types: `external`, `auto`, `manual`. Pick one; each block is a complete example of that choice.

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.init.identity {
  type = external
  certificates = [ ]
  identifier = "..."   # required
  # namespace = <string>
}
```

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.init.identity {
  type = auto
  identifier {
    type = random|explicit|config   # required
  }
}
```

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.init.identity {
  type = manual
  # takes no further keys
}
```

`identity.identifier` supports these types: `random`, `explicit`, `config`. Pick one; each block is a complete example of that choice.

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.init.identity.identifier {
  type = random
  # takes no further keys
}
```

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.init.identity.identifier {
  type = explicit
  name = "..."   # required
}
```

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.init.identity.identifier {
  type = config
  # takes no further keys
}
```


</Tab>
<Tab title="Table">

| Key | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `generate-intermediate-key` | boolean |  | `false` | If true (default false), then the node will generate an additional intermediate key. This allows to turn off access to the root key for the node. |
| `generate-topology-transactions-and-keys` | boolean |  | `true` | If true (default), then the node will generate automatically the topology transactions and keys once it has the necessary namespace delegations to do so. |
| **identity** |  |  |  | Control how the identity of the node is determined. |
|     `type` | one of `external`, `auto`, `manual` | yes | `auto` † | Choose one type — see [IdentityConfig](#identityconfig). Control how the identity of the node is determined. At startup, we need to determine the node's identity. We support various modes of operations for different deployments. We distinguish between setting the node-id and subsequently generating the topology transactions and the remaining keys. As such it is possible to manually set the node-id but let the system automatically generate the remaining topology transactions. |
| **identity** `type = external` |  |  |  | Read the node identifier from the config file |
|     `certificates` | array of File |  | `[]` | optional certificates to use (in case we have external root keys) |
|     `identifier` | string | yes |  | the string to use as identifier of the node |
|     `namespace` | string |  | _unset_ | optional fingerprint to use as the namespace (default is to extract it from the first certificate) |
| **identity** `type = auto` |  |  |  | Automatically generate a namespace key and initialize the node id |
|     **identifier** |  |  |  |  |
|         `type` | one of `random`, `explicit`, `config` | yes | `config` † | Choose one type — see [NodeIdentifierConfig](#nodeidentifierconfig). |
|     **identifier** `type = explicit` |  |  |  | Sets an explicit name for the node identifier |
|         `name` | string | yes |  | name to use as identifier |

</Tab>
</Tabs>

### `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.parameters`

<Tabs>
<Tab title="HOCON">

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.parameters {
  alpha-version-support = false
  beta-version-support = false
  delay-requests-before-lsu-traffic-init = false
  dev-version-support = false
  disable-aggregation-rule-size-check-for-testing = false
  disable-release-version-handshake-check = false
  disable-submission-checks-for-testing = false
  dont-warn-on-deprecated-pv = false
  enable-async-sequencer-logging = true
  enable-prevalidation = true
  enable-reject-delivered-aggregations-on-pv-35 = [ ]
  max-confirmation-requests-burst-factor = 0.5
  produce-post-ordering-topology-ticks = true
  unsafe-sequencer-channel-support = false
  async-writer {
    aggregation-batch-size = 1000
    block-info-batch-size = 1000
    traffic-batch-size = 1000
  }
  batching {
    # in-flight-aggregations-query-interval = <duration>
    ledger-api-pruning-batch-size = 50000
    max-acs-import-batch-size = 1000
    max-items-in-batch = 100
    max-pruning-batch-size = 1000
    max-pruning-time-interval = "30 minutes"
    max-stakeholder-groups-batch-size = 1000
    max-topology-update-batch-size = 1000
    max-topology-write-batch-size = 1000
    parallelism = 8
    pruning-parallelism = 2
    aggregator {
      type = batching|no-batching   # required
      maximum-batch-size = 500
    }
    contract-store-aggregator {
      type = batching|no-batching   # required
      maximum-batch-size = 500
    }
    mediator-fetch-finalized-responses-aggregator {
      type = batching|no-batching   # required
      maximum-batch-size = 500
    }
    mediator-store-finalized-responses-aggregator {
      type = batching|no-batching   # required
      maximum-batch-size = 500
    }
    topology-cache-aggregator {
      type = batching|no-batching   # required
      maximum-batch-size = 500
    }
  }
  caching {
    bft-ordering-batch-cache {
      expire-after-access = "1 minute"
      maximum-memory = "..."   # required
    }
    contract-store {
      expire-after-access = "1 minute"
      maximum-size = 0   # required
    }
    finalized-mediator-confirmation-requests {
      expire-after-access = "1 minute"
      maximum-size = 0   # required
    }
    indexed-strings {
      expire-after-access = "1 minute"
      maximum-size = 0   # required
    }
    key-cache {
      expire-after-access = "1 minute"
      maximum-size = 0   # required
    }
    kms-metadata-cache {
      expire-after-access = "1 minute"
      maximum-size = 0   # required
    }
    member-cache {
      expire-after-access = "1 minute"
      maximum-size = 0   # required
    }
    package-upgrade-cache {
      maximum-size = 0   # required
    }
    package-vetting-cache {
      expire-after-access = "1 minute"
      maximum-size = 0   # required
    }
    participant-cache {
      expire-after-access = "1 minute"
      maximum-size = 0   # required
    }
    party-cache {
      expire-after-access = "1 minute"
      maximum-size = 0   # required
    }
    public-key-conversion-cache {
      expire-after-access = "1 minute"
      maximum-size = 0   # required
    }
    sequencer-catchup-payload-cache {
      expire-after-access = "1 minute"
      maximum-memory = "..."   # required
    }
    sequencer-payload-cache {
      expire-after-access = "1 minute"
      maximum-memory = "..."   # required
    }
    session-encryption-key-cache {
      enabled = true
      receiver-cache {
        expire-after-timeout = "10 minutes"
        maximum-size = 0   # required
      }
      sender-cache {
        expire-after-timeout = "10 minutes"
        maximum-size = 0   # required
      }
    }
    synchronizer-client-max-timestamp {
      expire-after-access = "1 minute"
      maximum-size = 0   # required
    }
    topology-snapshot {
      expire-after-access = "1 minute"
      maximum-size = 0   # required
    }
  }
  lsu {
    contact-successor-retry {
      initial-delay = "30 seconds"   # required
      max-delay = "30 seconds"   # required
      max-retries = 0   # required
    }
  }
  lsu-repair {
    # global-max-sequencing-time-exclusive = <string>
    lsu-sequencing-bounds-override {
      lower-bound-sequencing-time-exclusive = "..."   # required
      upgrade-time = "..."   # required
    }
  }
  time-advancing-topology {
    grace-period = "500 milliseconds"
    max-sequencing-time-window = "30 seconds"
    poll-backoff = "250 milliseconds"
    sequencing-patience = "1 second"
  }
  watchdog {
    # check-interval = <duration>
    enabled = false   # required
    # kill-delay = <duration>
  }
}
```

`batching.aggregator` supports these types: `batching`, `no-batching`. Pick one; each block is a complete example of that choice.

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.parameters.batching.aggregator {
  type = batching
  maximum-batch-size = 500
  maximum-in-flight = 2
}
```

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.parameters.batching.aggregator {
  type = no-batching
  max-parallel-batches = 2
  maximum-batch-size = 500
}
```

| Key | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `alpha-version-support` **alpha** | boolean |  | `false` | if true, then support for alpha protocol versions will be turned on. |
| `beta-version-support` **beta** | boolean |  | `false` |  |
| `delay-requests-before-lsu-traffic-init` | boolean |  | `false` | Configures behavior of sendAsync requests until the traffic is initialized during LSU: If true, the sequencer will delay processing of the requests. If false, the sequencer will synchronously reject the requests with an error. |
| `dev-version-support` | boolean |  | `false` | if true, then support for the dev protocol version is enabled and the dev database schemas are applied, which does not provide data continuity guarantees and must not be used in production used for production. |
| `disable-aggregation-rule-size-check-for-testing` | boolean |  | `false` | Whether to disable the aggregation rule size check for testing purposes. This should only be used in tests and should eventually be removed as the tests are based on a feature that is not intended to be used in production. |
| `disable-release-version-handshake-check` | boolean |  | `false` | If true, then we won't check whether the client binaries are really supported during handshake. This is normally only useful for unstable protocol versions to avoid accidental ledger forks. |
| `disable-submission-checks-for-testing` | boolean |  | `false` | If true, disable checks on the write path of the sequencer in order to allow testing the same checks on the post-processing path (malicious sequencer node tests). Only to be used for testing purposes. |
| `dont-warn-on-deprecated-pv` | boolean |  | `false` | if true, then this sequencer will not emit a warning when configured to use protocol version 2.0.0. |
| `enable-async-sequencer-logging` | boolean |  | `true` | If true, then we will use the async logging with outcomes (but potentially out of order log messages) |
| `enable-prevalidation` | boolean |  | `true` | If true (as of 3.6), we will use pre-validation to move the signature validation into a separate parallel stage instead of the sequential step. |
| `enable-reject-delivered-aggregations-on-pv-35` | array of string |  | `MED` | No effect on pv34. On pv36 enabled for all node types. On pv35, the sequencer will reject aggregations that have already been completed eagerly for all configured member types, default is MED. |
| `max-confirmation-requests-burst-factor` | number (PositiveDouble) |  | `0.5` | how forgiving the rate limit is in case of bursts (so rate limit starts after observing an initial burst of factor * max_rate commands) |
| `produce-post-ordering-topology-ticks` | boolean |  | `true` | temporary flag to enable topology ticks produced post-ordering by sequencers (feature in development) |
| `unsafe-sequencer-channel-support` | boolean |  | `false` |  |

Important notes: - SHOULD be set only in disaster recovery scenarios. - MUST be the same value in all sequencers of a synchronizer. |
|     **lsu-sequencing-bounds-override** |  |  |  | Used to override values usually derived from the LSU announcement. |

|         `upgrade-time` | string (CantonTimestamp) | yes |  | The value should be chosen as follows: - At least lowerBoundSequencingTimeExclusive - At least the latest sequencing time on the broken synchronizer - At least the latest effective time of the latest non-rejected topology transaction on the broken synchronizer. - Not too far in the future, as it acts as a strict lower bound on the new synchronizer. |

|         `max-delay` | duration (NonNegativeDuration) | yes | `1 minute` † | max delay between retries |
|         `max-retries` | int | yes | `2147483647` † | max number of retries |

|     `grace-period` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) |  | `500 milliseconds` | Waiting period (measured on the local sequencer clock) on top of the topology change delay for the sequencer before it sends a broadcast message. If it processes another broadcast or topology transaction message during this waiting time whose sequencing time is after the effective timestamp, it will not send a broadcast message. A short grace period leads to faster observation of the topology transaction becoming effective; a long grace period reduces the number of broadcast messages sent. |
|     `max-sequencing-time-window` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) |  | `30 seconds` | The difference between the effective timestamp of the topology transaction and the max sequencing time for the time advancement broadcast message. The time advancement broadcast must be sequenced within this time window. |
|     `poll-backoff` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) |  | `250 milliseconds` | How frequently the sequencer should check whether it should send a broadcast message. Should be lower than the sequencingPatience and the gracePeriod. |
|     `sequencing-patience` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) |  | `1 second` | How long the sequencer waits before it resends a broadcast message for a topology change. Should be at least the expected latency of ordering a message. |

</Tabs>

### `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.public-api`

<Tabs>
<Tab title="HOCON">

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.public-api {
  address = "127.0.0.1"
  # flow-control-window = <int>
  initial-flow-control-window = 1048576
  max-auth-tokens-per-member = 25
  max-concurrent-calls-per-connection = 100000
  max-subscriptions-per-member = 5
  max-token-expiration-interval = "1 hour"
  max-token-lifetime = "Inf"
  nonce-expiration-interval = "1 minute"
  # override-max-request-size = <int>
  port = 0   # required
  use-exponential-random-token-expiration = false
  jwks-cache-config {
    auto-refresh-after = "0 seconds"
    cache-expiration = "5 minutes"
    cache-max-size = 1000
    connection-timeout = "10 seconds"
    read-timeout = "10 seconds"
  }
  keep-alive-server {
    permit-keep-alive-time = "20 seconds"
    permit-keep-alive-without-calls = false
    time = "40 seconds"
    timeout = "20 seconds"
  }
  limits {
    throttle-logging-rate-per-second = 10
    warn-on-undefined-limits = false
    active {
      <key> = [ ]
    }
  }
  tls {
    # ciphers = [ ... ]
    minimum-server-protocol-version = "TLSv1.2"
    private-key-file = "..."   # required
    cert-chain-file {
      type = pem-file|pem-string   # required
    }
  }
}
```

`tls.cert-chain-file` supports these types: `pem-file`, `pem-string`. Pick one; each block is a complete example of that choice.

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.public-api.tls.cert-chain-file {
  type = pem-file
  pem-file = "/path/to/file"   # required
}
```

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.public-api.tls.cert-chain-file {
  type = pem-string
  pem-bytes = "..."   # required
}
```


</Tab>
<Tab title="Table">

| Key | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `address` | string |  | `127.0.0.1` |  |
| `flow-control-window` | int (PositiveInt) |  | `ServerConfig.defaultFlowControlWindow` ‡ |  |
| `initial-flow-control-window` | int (PositiveInt) |  | `1048576` |  |
| `max-auth-tokens-per-member` | int (PositiveInt) |  | `25` | How many auth tokens can a member have concurrently. Older ones are being dropped. Generally, members should only have one or two (during renewal or restart) |
| `max-concurrent-calls-per-connection` | int (NonNegativeInt) |  | `100000` |  |
| `max-subscriptions-per-member` | int (PositiveInt) |  | `5` |  |
| `max-token-expiration-interval` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) |  | `1 hour` | Expiration time interval for authentication tokens. Tokens are used to authenticate participants. Choose a shorter interval for better security and a longer interval for better performance. |
| `max-token-lifetime` | duration (NonNegativeDuration) |  | `Inf` |  |
| `nonce-expiration-interval` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) |  | `1 minute` | Expiration interval for a nonce that is generated for an authentication challenge. As an authentication request is expected to be followed up with almost immediately to generate an authentication token the nonce expiry should be short. The nonce is automatically invalided on use. |
| `override-max-request-size` | int (NonNegativeInt) |  | _unset_ | overrides the default maximum request size in bytes on the sequencer node |
| `port` | int (Port) | yes |  |  |
| `use-exponential-random-token-expiration` | boolean |  | `false` | If enabled, the token expiration interval will be exponentially distributed with the following parameters: - `scale` of `0.75 * maxTokenExpirationInterval`; - value is re-sampled to fit into the interval [maxTokenExpirationInterval / 2, maxTokenExpirationInterval]. This is useful to avoid the thundering herd problem when many tokens expire at the same time and should result in nearly uniform distribution of token expiration intervals. If disabled, the token expiration interval will be constant. |
| **jwks-cache-config** |  |  |  | Configuration for jwks cache underpinning JWT token validation. |
|     `auto-refresh-after` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) |  | `0 seconds` |  |
|     `cache-expiration` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) |  | `5 minutes` |  |
|     `cache-max-size` | int |  | `1000` |  |
|     `connection-timeout` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) |  | `10 seconds` |  |
|     `read-timeout` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) |  | `10 seconds` |  |
| **keep-alive-server** |  |  |  |  |
|     `permit-keep-alive-time` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) |  | `20 seconds` |  |
|     `permit-keep-alive-without-calls` | boolean |  | `false` |  |
|     `time` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) |  | `40 seconds` |  |
|     `timeout` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) |  | `20 seconds` |  |
| **limits** |  |  |  | Configuration to limit the number of open requests per service |
|     `throttle-logging-rate-per-second` | int (NonNegativeInt) |  | `10` | maximum rate for logging rejections for requests exceeding the limit (prevents DOS on logs) |
|     `warn-on-undefined-limits` | boolean |  | `false` | emit warning if a limit is not configured for a stream |
|     **active** |  |  |  |  |
|         `<key>` | int (NonNegativeInt) |  | `[]` | map of service name to maximum number of parallel active requests or streams |
| **tls** |  |  |  | A wrapper for TLS server parameters supporting only server side authentication Same parameters as the more complete `TlsServerConfig` |
|     `ciphers` | array of string |  | `TlsConfig.defaultCiphers` ‡ |  |
|     `minimum-server-protocol-version` | string |  | `TLSv1.2` |  |
|     `private-key-file` | string (PemFile) | yes |  |  |
|     **cert-chain-file** |  |  |  | A class representing either an existing PEM file path or an inlined PEM string for configuration file fields |
|         `type` | one of `pem-file`, `pem-string` | yes |  | Choose one type — see [PemFileOrString](#pemfileorstring). A class representing either an existing PEM file path or an inlined PEM string for configuration file fields |
|     **cert-chain-file** `type = pem-file` |  |  |  | A class representing an existing PEM file path for configuration file fields |
|         `pem-file` | path (ExistingFile) | yes |  |  |
|     **cert-chain-file** `type = pem-string` |  |  |  | A class representing an inlined PEM string for configuration file fields |
|         `pem-bytes` | string | yes |  |  |

### `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.replication`

<Tabs>
<Tab title="HOCON">

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.replication {
  # enabled = <boolean>
  connection-pool {
    active-timeout = "15 seconds"
    health-check-period = "5 seconds"
    initial-locked-connection-timeout = "15 seconds"
    initial-must-remain-active-connection-timeout = "1 minute"
    connection {
      client-connection-check-interval = "5 seconds"
      connection-timeout = "10 seconds"
      health-check-period = "5 seconds"
      health-check-timeout = "15 seconds"
      initial-acquisition-interval = "200 milliseconds"
      initial-acquisition-max-retries = 5
      keep-alive-count = 5
      keep-alive-idle = "10 seconds"
      keep-alive-interval = "1 second"
      max-inconclusive-read-only-checks = 3
      passive-check-period = "15 seconds"
      lock {
        health-check-period = "5 seconds"
        health-check-timeout = "15 seconds"
      }
    }
  }
}
```


</Tab>
<Tab title="Table">

| Key | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `enabled` | boolean |  | _unset_ | Needs to be set to true for a replicated node, which shares the database with other replicas. |
| **connection-pool** |  |  |  | Configuration for the connection pool using DB locks. |
|     `active-timeout` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) |  | `15 seconds` | Time to wait until the first connection in the pool becomes active during failover. |
|     `health-check-period` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) |  | `5 seconds` | Health check period, i.e., how long to wait between health checks of the connection pool. |
|     `initial-locked-connection-timeout` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) |  | `15 seconds` | Time to wait until the initial connection is either active or passive (external sequencer does not use this) |
|     `initial-must-remain-active-connection-timeout` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) |  | `1 minute` | If using an external sequencer we want to make sure it is always active, unless it is closing. This timeout is the initial time to wait before first becoming active, otherwise it is shutdown. |
|     **connection** |  |  |  | Configuration of a DB-locked connection, i.e., a database connection with an associated DB lock. |
|         `client-connection-check-interval` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) |  | `5 seconds` | Interval for client connection checks. Corresponds to the Postgres `client_connection_check_interval` configuration parameter (Postgres &gt;= 14 only). Millisecond granularity is the lowest supported precision. `None` leaves the server default unchanged whereas a Some(0) explicitly disables the check. On macOS with Postgres 14, this must be disabled, since in Postgres 14, this setting requires the non-standard `POLLRDHUP` extension to the `poll` system call, which is only available on Linux. See html for details. |
|         `connection-timeout` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) |  | `10 seconds` | Timeout for requesting a new connection from the underlying database driver in seconds granularity. |
|         `health-check-period` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) |  | `5 seconds` | Health check period, i.e., how long to wait between one health check completed and the next to start. |
|         `health-check-timeout` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) |  | `15 seconds` | Timeout for running a health check in seconds granularity. |
|         `initial-acquisition-interval` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) |  | `200 milliseconds` | Retry intervals during the initial attempts to acquire the lock. |
|         `initial-acquisition-max-retries` | int |  | `5` | Maximum number of retries when trying to acquire the lock for the first time before trying to acquire the lock in a background task. |
|         `keep-alive-count` | int |  | `5` | TCP keep-alive count, i.e., how many unanswered keep-alive messages required to consider the connection lost. |
|         `keep-alive-idle` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) |  | `10 seconds` | TCP keep-alive idle time, i.e., how long to wait until sending a keep-alive message when idle. |
|         `keep-alive-interval` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) |  | `1 second` | TCP keep-alive interval, i.e., how long to wait until resending an unanswered keep-alive message. |
|         `max-inconclusive-read-only-checks` | int |  | `3` | Number of consecutive inconclusive (timed-out or failed) read-only checks tolerated before the connection is considered lost. A single inconclusive check can be due to transient contention, but a persistently inconclusive check is problematic. |
|         `passive-check-period` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) |  | `15 seconds` | How long to wait between trying to become active. |
|         **lock** |  |  |  | Configuration of a DB lock |
|             `health-check-period` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) |  | `5 seconds` | Health check period, i.e., how long to wait between one health check completed and the next to start. |
|             `health-check-timeout` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) |  | `15 seconds` | Timeout for running a health check in seconds granularity. |

</Tab>
</Tabs>

### `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.sequencer-client`

<Tabs>
<Tab title="HOCON">

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.sequencer-client {
  acknowledgement-interval = "1 minute"
  amplify-on-max-sequencing-time-too-far = true
  # channel-flow-control-window = <int>
  channel-initial-flow-control-window = 1048576
  channel-max-inbound-message-size = 134217728
  default-max-sequencing-time-offset = "2 minutes"
  enable-amplification-improvements = true
  event-inbox-size = 100
  handshake-retry-attempts = 50
  handshake-retry-delay = "5 seconds"
  initial-connection-retry-delay = "10 milliseconds"
  max-connection-retry-delay = "30 seconds"
  maximum-in-flight-event-batches = 20
  # override-max-request-size = <int>
  past-events-cache-size = 1000
  skip-sequenced-event-validation = false
  startup-connection-retry-delay = "1 second"
  time-readings-retention = "5 minutes"
  use-new-aggregator = true
  use-new-connection-pool = true
  warn-disconnect-delay = "10 seconds"
  auth-token {
    min-retry-interval = "500 milliseconds"
    refresh-auth-token-before-expiry = "20 seconds"
    retries = 20
    backoff {
      base = 2
      max-retry-interval = "2 minutes"
      jitter {
        type = equal|full   # required
      }
    }
  }
  keep-alive-client {
    idle-timeout = "30 minutes"
    keep-alive-without-calls = false
    time = "40 seconds"
    timeout = "15 seconds"
  }
}
```


</Tab>
<Tab title="Table">

| Key | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `acknowledgement-interval` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) |  | `1 minute` | Controls how frequently the client acknowledges how far it has successfully processed to the sequencer which allows the sequencer to remove this data when pruning. |
| `amplify-on-max-sequencing-time-too-far` | boolean |  | `true` |  |
| `channel-flow-control-window` | int (PositiveInt) |  | `ClientChannelParams.DefaultFlowControlWindow` ‡ | switches to manual gRPC flow control and sets its window; if `None`, then it is not configured and the implementation default is used. Switches to automatic gRPC flow control and sets its initial window; if `None`, then it is not |
| `channel-initial-flow-control-window` | int (PositiveInt) |  | `1048576` | Switches to automatic gRPC flow control and sets its initial window; if `None`, then it is not configured and the implementation default is used. If present, it is set after the `flowControlWindow` parameter, so it overrides it. |
| `channel-max-inbound-message-size` | int (NonNegativeInt) |  | `134217728` | max inbound request size for the grpc channel to the sequencer. |
| `default-max-sequencing-time-offset` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) |  | `2 minutes` | if no max-sequencing-time is supplied to send, our current time will be offset by this amount |
| `enable-amplification-improvements` | boolean |  | `true` | Enables some improvements in the amplification behavior: - When sending a submission request with amplification, if we receive a synchronous error from the sequencer that is classified as `RequestRefused`, we abort amplification and bubble up the error to the caller. If this parameter is enabled, in the specific case of a sequencer refusing the submission because it is overloaded (Overloaded), we will immediately continue to the next step of amplification. - Scheduling of the next amplification takes into account the time taken by the transport to provide a response. |
| `event-inbox-size` | int (PositiveInt) |  | `100` | The size of the inbox queue used to store received events. Must be at least one. Events in the inbox are processed in parallel. A larger inbox may result in higher throughput at the price of higher memory consumption, larger database queries, and longer crash recovery. |
| `handshake-retry-attempts` | int (NonNegativeInt) |  | `50` | How many attempts should we make to get a handshake response |
| `handshake-retry-delay` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) |  | `5 seconds` | How long to delay between attempts to fetch a handshake response |
| `initial-connection-retry-delay` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) |  | `10 milliseconds` | Initial delay before a reconnect attempt |
| `max-connection-retry-delay` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) |  | `30 seconds` | Maximum delay before a reconnect attempt |
| `maximum-in-flight-event-batches` | int (PositiveInt) |  | `20` | The maximum number of event batches that the system will process concurrently. Setting the `maximumInFlightEventBatches` parameter limits the number of event batches that the system will process simultaneously, preventing overload and ensuring that the system can handle the workload effectively. A higher value of `maximumInFlightEventBatches` can lead to increased throughput, but at the cost of higher memory consumption and longer processing times for each batch. A lower value of `maximumInFlightEventBatches` may limit throughput, but can result in more stable and predictable system behavior. |
| `override-max-request-size` | int (NonNegativeInt) |  | _unset_ | overrides the maxRequestSize configured in the dynamic synchronizer parameters. If overrideMaxRequestSize is set, modifying the maxRequestSize won't have any effect. This is only used for testing. |
| `past-events-cache-size` | int (PositiveInt) |  | `1000` | If a subscription receives an event for aggregation which has already been decided, a cache of past successfully aggregated events is used to determine whether this is legitimate (possibly due to load or catching up) or not. Only the pastEventsCacheSize most recent events are kept in the cache. Must be at least 1. Larger values reduce the likelihood of falsely classifying an old event as illegitimate, at the cost of more memory consumption. Only effective if useNewAggregator is enabled. |
| `skip-sequenced-event-validation` | boolean |  | `false` | if true, sequenced event validation will be skipped. the default setting is false. this option should only be enabled if a defective validation is blocking processing. therefore, unless you know what you are doing, you shouldn't touch this setting. |
| `startup-connection-retry-delay` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) |  | `1 second` | Initial delay before we attempt to establish an initial connection |
| `time-readings-retention` | duration (PositiveFiniteDuration) |  | `5 minutes` | The duration for which sequencing time readings are retained. This setting depends on the assumptions about dynamic faults and ensures that faulty time readings skewed towards the future do not linger in the system for too long. Regular eviction also ensures that time readings from offboarded sequencers are not taken into account indefinitely. |
| `use-new-aggregator` | boolean |  | `true` | Use the new BFT sequencer aggregator instead of the former one. |
| `use-new-connection-pool` | boolean |  | `true` | Use the new sequencer connection pool instead of the former transports. |
| `warn-disconnect-delay` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) |  | `10 seconds` | Consider sequencer to be degraded after delay |
| **auth-token** |  |  |  | Configures authentication token fetching |
|     `min-retry-interval` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) |  | `500 milliseconds` |  |
|     `refresh-auth-token-before-expiry` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) |  | `20 seconds` | how much time before the auth token expires should we fetch a new one? |
|     `retries` | int (NonNegativeInt) |  | `20` |  |
|     **backoff** |  |  |  |  |
|         `base` | int (NonNegativeInt) |  | `2` |  |
|         `max-retry-interval` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) |  | `2 minutes` |  |
|         **jitter** |  |  |  |  |
|             `type` | one of `equal`, `full` | yes |  |  |
| **keep-alive-client** |  |  |  | gRPC keep alive client configuration Settings according to TimeUnit- |
|     `idle-timeout` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) |  | `30 minutes` | If there are no RPCs during this duration, the channel will close all connections and switch to Idle state (default is 30 min) |
|     `keep-alive-without-calls` | boolean |  | `false` | Enables sending of keep alive even when there are no RPCs on the channel (default to false). This increases network resource consumption for idle connections and requires the server to enable permitKeepAliveWithoutCalls. Prefer using idleTimeout. |
|     `time` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) |  | `40 seconds` | Sets the time without read activity before sending a keepalive ping. Do not set to small numbers (default is 40s) |
|     `timeout` | duration (NonNegativeFiniteDuration) |  | `15 seconds` | Sets the time waiting for read activity after sending a keepalive ping (default is 15s). The default timeout was previously 20s and was lowered to 15s, because besides configuring the gRPC KeepAliveManager, it also sets up the socket's TCP_USER_TIMEOUT (see html). 15s gives a larger margin to detect a faulty connection earlier and retry a submission on another sequencer via amplification, thereby avoiding a request failure. |

</Tab>
</Tabs>

### `canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.sequencer`

<Tabs>
<Tab title="HOCON">

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.sequencer {
  type = database|external|bft-sequencer   # required
}
```

`` supports these types: `database`, `external`, `bft-sequencer`. Pick one; each block is a complete example of that choice.

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.sequencer {
  type = database
  # testing-interceptor = <string>
  high-availability {
    # enabled = <boolean>
    keep-alive-interval = "50 milliseconds"
    offline-duration = "8 seconds"
    online-check-interval = "5 seconds"
    total-node-count = 10
    connection-pool {
      active-timeout = "15 seconds"
      health-check-period = "5 seconds"
      initial-locked-connection-timeout = "15 seconds"
      initial-must-remain-active-connection-timeout = "1 minute"
      connection {
        client-connection-check-interval = "5 seconds"
        connection-timeout = "10 seconds"
        health-check-period = "5 seconds"
        health-check-timeout = "15 seconds"
        initial-acquisition-interval = "200 milliseconds"
        initial-acquisition-max-retries = 5
        keep-alive-count = 5
        keep-alive-idle = "10 seconds"
        keep-alive-interval = "1 second"
        max-inconclusive-read-only-checks = 3
        passive-check-period = "15 seconds"
        lock {
          health-check-period = "5 seconds"
          health-check-timeout = "15 seconds"
        }
      }
    }
    exclusive-storage {
      max-connections = 2
      connection-pool {
        active-timeout = "15 seconds"
        health-check-period = "5 seconds"
        initial-locked-connection-timeout = "15 seconds"
        initial-must-remain-active-connection-timeout = "1 minute"
        connection {
          client-connection-check-interval = "5 seconds"
          connection-timeout = "10 seconds"
          health-check-period = "5 seconds"
          health-check-timeout = "15 seconds"
          initial-acquisition-interval = "200 milliseconds"
          initial-acquisition-max-retries = 5
          keep-alive-count = 5
          keep-alive-idle = "10 seconds"
          keep-alive-interval = "1 second"
          max-inconclusive-read-only-checks = 3
          passive-check-period = "15 seconds"
          lock {
            health-check-period = "5 seconds"
            health-check-timeout = "15 seconds"
          }
        }
      }
    }
  }
  pruning {
    max-pruning-batch-size = 50000
    # pruning-metric-update-interval = <duration>
    traffic-purchased-retention = "1 hour"
  }
  reader {
    checkpoint-interval = "5 seconds"
    event-generation-parallelism = 4
    payload-batch-size = 10
    payload-batch-window = "5 milliseconds"
    payload-fetch-parallelism = 2
    # polling-interval = <duration>
    read-batch-size = 100
  }
  writer {
    type = low-latency|high-throughput   # required
  }
}
```

```hocon
canton.sequencers.<sequencer>.sequencer {
  type = external
  config = "..."   # required
  sequencer-type = "..."   # required
  block {
    # testing-interceptor = <string>
    circuit-breaker {
      enabled = true
      messages {
        acknowledgement {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
        commitment {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
        confirmation-request {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
        confirmation-response {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
        lsu-sequencing-test {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
        time-proof {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
        top-up {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
        topology {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
        unexpected {
          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"

        }

          allowed-block-delay = "90 seconds"
          exponential-backoff-factor = 1
          max-failures = 10
          max-reset-timeout = "36500 days"
          reset-timeout = "30 seconds"
        }
      }
    }
    reader {
      checkpoint-interval = "5 seconds"
      event-generation-parallelism = 4
      payload-batch-size = 10
      payload-batch-window = "5 milliseconds"
      payload-fetch-parallelism = 2
      # polling-interval = <duration>
      read-batch-size = 100
    }
    stream-instrumentation {
      buffer-size = 256
      is-enabled = false
    }
    throughput-cap {
      clock-tick-interval = "100 milliseconds"
      delayed-activation = true
      enabled = false
      observation-period-seconds = 60
      strict = true
      thresholds = [ ]
      update-every-ms = 100
      messages {
        confirmation-request {
          global-kbps-cap = 2000
          global-tps-cap = 10
          per-client-kbps-cap = 1000
          per-client-tps-cap = 4
        }
        confirmation-response {
          global-kbps-cap = 2000
          global-tps-cap = 10
          per-client-kbps-cap = 1000
          per-client-tps-cap = 4
        }
        topology {
          global-kbps-cap = 2000
          global-tps-cap = 10
          per-client-kbps-cap = 1000
          per-client-tps-cap = 4
        }
      }
    }
    writer {
      type = low-latency|high-throughput   # required
    }
  }
}
```
